## Firmal

Firmal was a musician member of the Heart of Bane and part of the Bard's Guild of Ravens Bluff in the late 14 century DR.

History
By Uktar of the, Firmal found his calling as a member of a band that performed in Ravens Bluff. The musician's instrument of choice was something called illithid bagpipes. Firmal found like-minded performers among Everett Taber who played drums of panic, Havoc Sen Thornclaw with the ax of deafening and destruction used as an instrument, Vanduhar Gerenstalt Eisner‎ and his Illmataran organ of pain, all gathered under the leadership of the Kossuthan priest and the band's lead wailer named Nassar. As a group, they picked a name that would prove highly problematic. The Heart of Bane band was named after the infamous relic of the same name. In Uktar, the Bard's Guild plastered the city with posters that advertised that the Heart of Bane would be making an appearance at a performance aimed at helping the city's morale.

Unsurprisingly, the ill-conceived advertisement attracted the attention of a band of tanar'ri led by a marilith, as the planar being were attracted to the relic's dark magic, plaguing the city at the time. The ensuing slaughter ended the lives of a dozen innocents, and the Bard's Guild was lit aflame, with the guild being severely damaged. The demonic attack was ended by a Golden Rooster Patrol led by Sir Kirth Sonath-Pann. In the fallout of the performance, Firmal and the Heart of Bane band went into hiding, leaving only a spokesperson to proclaim their innocence. The proceedings from the show were never paid to Firmal's group as the Bard's Guild claimed that the box office was ransacked by a nabassu.

## Fig

Figs were edible fruits found in warmer climate nations of Faerûn and in further, among the nations of Kara-Tur and Zakhara.

Cultivation
Figs were known to be a sweet edible fruit as early as the ancient empire of Netheril.

Fresh figs were grown in the West Faerûn nation of Calimshan and often consumed with flatbreads along with prunes, dates, dried peas, and goat cheese.

In a similar fashion, in Sembia, figs were parts of feasts, served alongside other fruits and sweet-tasting meats, cheeses, white bread, butter, and pickled eggs. In the nearby kingdom of Cormyr, dried figs were available, namely, in the town of Marsember.

Dannath's Pickles, Nuts, & Foods in the city of Yartar sold dried southern fruits, including figs.

Just south of Riatavin in the Lands of Intrigue was located a rich Rhianna estate from which Thousandheads Trading Coster operated. The estate's grounds held massive acreages of fig and date orchards. Sunrise Orchards was another notable location that grew fig trees. Fresh figs were also a staple of Turmian cuisine.

Wild fig trees grew in the hot sands of Anauroch around the five Colored Waters lakes. The fruits were eaten raw, served with roasted hare, and dried to be eaten as rations.

In the southern land of Halruaa, sugared figs were a common sweet treat.

Sweet fig treats were served in Eltabbar for the Zulkirs and those who held power in Thay. Mulhorand and Great Vale cultivated fruits, including figs.

Figs were consumed by the Laska people of the Kilap Valley in Shou Lung during feasts of roast boar and fresh papayas.

People of the Haunted Lands in Zakhara cultivated dates, figs, and pomegranates. Figs were a commonly sold commodity in Tajar of the Pearl Cities, and grew in abundance on the isles of Masud Jazayir.

Usages
Calimshan was known for its figs, pressed into a flat oval shape – a treat simply called fig cakes.

In North Faerûn, honeyed figs were among some of the most common preserves, kept in wax-sealed jars.

Trivia
Calishites made a hand gesture called the fig sign by placing a thumb between the middle fingers. That sign was believed to ward evil names off.

## Fimril

Fimril of Westgate was a mageling of the Zhentarim in the mid-14 century DR.

Description
He had dark eyes and wore a black robe.

Personality
To Sarhthor, like most magelings of the Zhentarim, Fimril was dark-hearted and ambitious. Though he bore a spark of self-doubt, he was brave, arrogant, and indeed ambitious, believing himself capable of rising to the top of the Zhentarim, and considering that people who talked back to him in a way he disapproved of could be done without.

Abilities
Fimril was able to use a slaying spell that consumed a crushed black pearl as its material component, almost certainly death spell. His version of the spell, however, was strong enough to kill him and his comrades too. He had also devised a unique spell that took him an entire year to construct, which created a moving barrier of all-devouring darkness, a spell powerful enough to counter spellfire.

Another strange power of Fimril was a very draining spell that called up images from the ashes of a fire, allowing a mage to see what had transcurred while it was burning.

Relationships
He'd been either trained or tested by Sarhthor. Lyrkon, Glondar, and Duthspurn were both part of the squad to which he was assigned.

History
Prior to, Fimril was Fimril of Westgate, strongly implying he'd spent a significant amount of time in that city. He'd spent at least one year, per his own recollection, developing a spell that created an all-devouring shield of darkness, capable of stopping most spells and disintegrating most material that it went through.

He was part of a meeting in late, in which Sarhthor offered several magelings of the Zhentarim the assignment to hunt down Shandril Shessair for her spellfire. He caught up with her at Hullack Forest, where he was able to magically examine a campfire and scry on what she'd done. During their confrontation, he used a novel spell that created an all-disintegrating screen of darkness; by moving it, he kept his forces protected from spellfire, and then used the screen to threaten Delg Hammerhand's life, successfully getting Shandril to surrender.

Though he captured Shandril Shessair, he used a killing spell on Mirt when the latter turned up to rescue her; the spell was reflected, slaying him.

## Fireforges

Fireforges was a realm of industrious dwarves in east Faerûn, home to the Arforge, Hulhammer, and Tamstone clans.

Geography
The realm lay in the roots of Mount Thulbane, extending beneath parts of Threskel and Chessenta. The region was highly volcanic, with warm and humid caverns, magma rivers, and dangerous gas pockets.

Trade
As of the 15 century DR, the clans of Fireforges mined widely and aggressively, coming into conflict over mining claims with Khaelud to the south. The clans took advantage of the volcanism to power their smelting and forging, producing great amounts of metalwork that they exported.

## Fireleap Way

Fireleap Way was a long and straight street in Ravens Bluff that crossed the city almost completely, from east to west, beginning in the Foreign District and ending in the Tentowers neighborhood.

Geography
Fireleap Way's western end was located in the Foreign District, by the Coker Wharf Company's docks. From there the street ran east, turning into Daren's Ring circle with the Ring Park in the middle, turning back into a straight line just before the gate out of the Foreign District. Next, Fireleap Way passed the moot with Westwall Way North and Fire Lane, further east it crossed Raphiel Road, Blacktree Boulevard North, DeVillars Ride, Manycoins Way, the moot with Macintyre Path and Evensong Ride, followed by crossings with O'Kane Court, Waelstar Way, and Lamorgyr's Ride. Then, Fireleap Way crossed DeVillars Creek via the Old Dragonbridge, and continued on east past Torngel's Ride, Stonekeep Concourse, and finally ended on Bouldergrinder's Way.

Notable Locations
Embassy of Tantras, just where Fireleap Way began and turned into Daren's Ring.
Embassy of Thay, on the southern side of the street, across from the Embassy of Tantras.
Home of Hark the Herald, on Daren's Ring's southeast.
Hangman's Courthouse, a large plaza, south of where Westwall Way North and Fire Lane crossed Fireleap Way.
Littleate & Sons, a moneylender located on the crossing of Fireleap Way and Blacktree Boulevard North.
Ondrel Spires, a large luxury apartments complex located just past Littleate & Sons.
The Stout Staff, a vendor of canes, crutches, and peglegs located on the crossing with Manycoins Way.
Tower of Holy Revelry, a temple dedicated to Lliira, located across Fireleap Way from The Stout Staff.
Home of Regora Ravensworth, on the moot with Macintyre Path and Evensong Ride.
Ravengard House, the manse home of the Therogeon family, facing Lamorgyr's Ride.
Stane Opera House, across the street from the Ravengard House, facing Lamorgyr's Ride.
House of the Rose, a merchant house located on the crossing with Torngel's Ride.
Gurneth's Goblet eatery on Bouldergrinder's Way.

## Fisherling

Fisherlings were thieves—generally children—active in the Malar's Throat slums of Port Nyanzaru as of the late 15 century DR.

Activities
Fisherlings were so-named for their tactic of "fishing" for unsuspecting people's coin purses and other valuables from the high suspension bridges that spanned the Malar's Throat ravine. They would skillfully dangle a fishhook from a long line or wire—sometimes from hundreds of feet above ground level—to steal possessions directly from victims' belts. They were known to target individuals wearing unsecured items and satchels that appeared to be carrying valuables. If spotted by their victims, they gleefully reeled in their lines and scattered into the slums, taking cover in the crowds.

History
The fisherlings were a well-known hazard of visiting Malar's Throat as of the late 15 century DR.

## Firefruit

Firefruit, or amberglow, was a sweet berry that grew every three to four years on certain species of flowering mosses and lichens in North Faerûn.

When
seen, protruding from hair-thin stalks out of the scabrous fungi, they can be eaten with surety,
because there's nothing poisonous or inedible that looks like firefruit.

Description
Firefruits were small, tough-skinned berries with their centers filled with gooey and sweet like honey flesh. These berries grew on hair-thin stalks. Most mosses and lichens in the Savage Frontier never flowered, and it took the most experienced rangers and expert foresters to know how to identify the berry-bearing vegetation. However, when found, amberglow could be eaten without any reservations as the berries were edible and there were no poisonous berries that could be mistaken for them.

## Firewater

Firewater was a spell of alteration and the alchemy school of thaumaturgy that allowed novice wizards to create a flammable substance.

Effects
Casting this spell would transform a volume of water into a substance similar to alcohol, but lighter than water. The amount of volume that could be transformed was  or more according to the caster's capability, with the highest possible volume being .

This firewater substance was volatile and flammable, bursting into hot flames at even a spark that lasted for one minute. However, the substance had to be used within a minute of its creation, as otherwise it would evaporate. This would occur even if the substance was in a securely sealed container.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, casting this spell required a material component in the form of a few grains of sugar and a raisin.

Notable Users
 Greater sea hags commonly included this spell among their repertoire.
 Peppercorn, a faerie dragon who lived in Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR, knew how to cast firewater.
 Red Wizards of Thay that specialized in alteration commonly knew this spell.

Finder was a descendant of Therion Gers surreptitiously given a serpentskin tome by Terraseer which he used to explore Aryvandaar ruins while an apprentice in Glaurachyndaar.

He found the scrolls in  while searching cellars of the ruins that would later be rebuilt into the fortress Citadel of the Mists west of the Far Horns Forest . ## First Dynasty of the Moonshaes

The First Dynasty was a Talfiric noble family from Ebenfar that served as chieftains of the first humans to settle in the Moonshae Isles, becoming the first of the Ffolk.

History
The First Dynasty were descendants of the Archduke Markus Randol of Ebenfar. When tribes of Talfir came to be prosecuted by Ebenfar's Shadowking in the, Randol's daughter, Gwynneth, led the tribes west across the Sea of Swords to settle in the Moonshae Isles. They were said to have first arrived on Alaron but founded their first settlement on the large southern island which would be named in Gwynneth's honor. The site they chose would come to be known as Caer Corwell, but soon other cantrevs were founded as well. The first settlers rapidly built fortifications in anticipation of being pursued by their enemies, however no such enemies ever arrived. They were soon joined by additional waves of migration as word of their freedom spread.

Gwynneth died soon after her arrival in the Isles, and was succeeded in the, by her consort, Taylor, who would die in battle when the settlers came into conflict with the local Llewyrr elves in the . After five years of conflict, Gwynneth and Taylor's son, Deric, successfully united the humans to found the kingdom of Corwell in the . By this time, the Talfiric settlers had already come to be known as the "Ffolk", which simply meant "the people" in their language.

While King Deric managed to unite the Ffolk and to establish peace with the elves during his two decades of rule, he died without an heir. This ended the First Dynasty and precipitated a six-year civil war among the Ffolk, which only ended with the victory of Callidyrr Hugh, who founded the Hugh Dynasty.

Notable Members
 Gwynneth "the First Mother", who led the refugees to the Moonshaes.
 Taylor, Gwynneth's consort and successor.
 King Deric, the son and heir of Gwynneth and Taylor.

Five (Or Six) Heroes was a book written in simple and easy to understand style aimed at the young. ## Fine Fabrics

Fine Fabrics was a clothier shop in the Upper City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Services
Fine Fabrics did business with the Upper City patriars and city governors, outfitting them with quality finery. The shop's services were available in exchange for coin or letters of credit.

History
In the, Gerard Densim, the proprietor of Fine Fabrics, did business with the Guild, paid for the organization's thugs to stand guard over the shop, protecting it from the dreaded Stone Lord, a new kingpin allied with the Cult of the Absolute. As the guild war grew more severe, The Guild Bursar Uktar raised the protection tithes. If unpaid, the guard would be withdrawn from Fine Fabrics. Gerard wrote a politely pleading letter stating he could not afford the guard to be removed and asked if it would be a good compromise for the Guild to accept a letter of credit from Fine Fabrics in addition to a twenty-percent discount for the Guild's guard (so they could look presentable while standing guard) and a fifty-percent discount to the guildmaster Nine-Fingers Keene, the city's fifth and unforgotten duke.

## Firelia Jannath

Lady Firelia Wisteria Jannath, or simply Lady Jannath, was a high social status member of the patriar Jannath family of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.This article treats Firelia Wisteria Jannath as a single character, a likely result of the game's rewrites. Lady Jannath is described to be middle-aged when inspecting her portrait painted by Oskar Fevras, while in in-game dialogues Enver Gortash is described to be young and handsome (despite his model being middle-aged). A note found in Lady Jannath's vault describes Wisteria Jannath's past affair with Gortash. Lady Jannath's age allows for the said timeline; however, it makes it less likely considering Lady Jannath's middle age and, consequently, her mother's advanced age. Lady Jannath's parents are both mentioned in-game but remain unnamed, and their mention is present in the present-time context of Lady Jannath's long-awaited marriage. With Lady Jannath's mother being alive and not inhabiting the same Lower City mansion, it would be a fair assumption that Lady Jannath's personal vault key in her possession contains her own personal and secret possessions, not the possessions of a family member. Additionally, a note where Lady Jannath withdraws her engagement ring from the Counting House confirms once again that she is the vault's owner.

Description
In the late 15 century DR, Lady Jannath was a beautiful middle-aged woman with hair of autumn red and green eyes. Her light freckled skin showed early signs of aging, but her elegant appearance, fashionable jewelry, and green gowns were enough to highlight the woman's attractiveness.

Relationships
Lady Jannath once shared a romantic entanglement with Lord Enver Gortash. In the years that followed, many men sought her hand in marriage, but the man she ended up marrying was an artist and painter of some renown. Oskar Fevras was courted by Lady Jannath and eventually left another woman for the patriar. Despite rumors and bad faith arguments, Oskar did feel affection for Wisteria, as his paintings of her clearly showed.

When it was believed her husband perished, several men attempted for the Lady's hand and affections: Lord Haventh, Jonah, and Horatius, among others.

Possessions
Wisteria Jannath did not live in the family's Upper City Jannath Estate, instead, she owned a mansion in the Lower City.

Lady Jannath wore an enchanted wedding band – Til Death Do Us Part, that allowed her to cast the beacon of hope spell. She also owned a powerful magic headwear – Jannath's hat, which she kept locked away in the safety of a vast in the Counting House, alongside a diary from her lavender-perfumed love affair.

Activities
Lady Jannath was a patron of the arts and hosted art shows at her Lower City estate. One of the upcoming showings as of 1492 DR was "Strange Tides: Self-Portraits from the Sword Coast," with patriars from other noble families among the attendees. Smallbites and libations for the event included wild mushroom soup, steak medallions with asparagus, and golden pear pie.

History
At some point before the, Lady Jannath had a brief but passionate affair with Enver Gortash, whose mere hugs made Wisteria feel like a young debutante. In her passion, Wisteria gifted Lord Gortash a priceless diamond ring – a heirloom of House Jannath worth more than her entire mansion.

Eventually, Lady Jannath found a new object of desire. She began mutual courting with the artist Oskar Fevras, who was commissioned to paint her portrait, subsequently wresting his affections from the woman he thought to be the love of his life once. The affair quickly blossomed into a betrothal and marriage. The ceremony was generously financed by Lady Jannath's parents with the High Hall being discussed as one of the venues. When Oskar disappeared on Kythorn 13 of 1492 DR, Lady Jannath at first assumed he was consumed by his art. Eventually, it became quite obvious something had happened. Wisteria asked her butler – dragonborn Tarhun Mnemonis, to discretely ask around about the missing painter. By Kythorn 18, she knew that something had to be done as the painter had been missing for five days. Lady Jannath made a hefty donation to the Flaming Fists to ensure they were aware of the case's urgency. Lastly, she hired the Blue Lynx Adventurers troupe for 1,000 gp with an additional charge of 1,000 gp for incidentals to track the painter down. They returned empty-handed – no trace of Oskar from Baldur's Gate to Waterdeep. The truth of the matter was Oskar left the city for the open air of the countryside to clear his mind after the scandal broke out in the Gate over the news of Oskar's affair and marriage to Lady Jannath.

Unfortunately, with news of Oskar's exploits out, he made a ripe target for blackmailers and other criminals. He was abducted by a Zhentarim slaver named Brem and taken to the Zhentarim hideout underneath the Waukeen's Rest inn. One way or another, Oskar ended up escaping slavery, either with help from a group of adventurers known as the Absolute Adversaries or with some desperate gull.

However, after his return, the painter started losing appetite, getting depressed, and eventually was revealed to be possessed by the spirit of his other love – Kerri Evenfield, who took her own life after being left by Oskar. The possessed painter flooded Lady Jannath's Estate with cursed apparitions and ghosts, scaring away guests and servants. Lady Jannath remained with the belligerent possessed artist and tried to break through to her husband. Unbeknownst to all but the painter, the source of possession was the portrait of Kerri, Oskar hid in his studio in the mansion's attic. Tormented by guilt Oskar hired Mystic Carrion to seek forgiveness from his dead love, but enraged and undead Kerri had different plans. On an order from Oskar, Mystic Carrion also procured a rare magic item – a torch of revocation, the only thing that could destroy the painting that tethered vengeful Kerri Evenfield to the Prime Material plane. The painting may had been destroyed by the Absolute Adversaries, earning them Lady Jannath's gratitude.

## Fiendish graft

Fiendish grafts were body parts of fiends that were attached to the bodies of other corporeal creatures or people.

Description
These grafts were inherently malevolent, and having one would corrupt a person's personality as well as their appearance. Recipients of such grafts found themselves either tempted toward committing evil acts or being driven mad if such urges conflicted with their goodly nature. These changes made them noticeably less trustworthy to nonevil beings. Despite this, fiendish grafts were technically not magical, nor did they have sentience. They could not generally be salvaged from the creatures to whom they were attached.

Creation
Only fiends possessed the knowledge of how to create and attach such grafts, and the process generally required the completion of a dark ritual. Melding fiends' body parts onto another creature usually required familiarity with medicine, specialized knowledge of how to graft flesh, and often access to one or more relevant spells. Two known exceptions to these rules inluded fiends of corruption—who had the power to freely bestow grafts as rewards or temptations for mortals whom they were trying to corrupt or tempt into infernal contracts—and sibriexes—who could conjure grafts from Abyssal chaos and instantly meld one onto a willing recipient or force one onto a helpless victim.

There were also said to exist magical devices into which a creature could be placed that performed fiendish grafting. Limbs were automatically removed and replaced in exceedingly painful—and potentially fatal—procedures.

Notable Fiendish Grafts
Arms
Clawed arm
A muscled and sinewy arm with a large, clawed hand.
Flexible arm
A long, strong, and tentacle-like arm that ended in a clawed hand with three fingers.
Grappling tentacle
A long, strong, and tentacle-like arm that ended in a barbed hook ideal for grabbing onto enemies.
Long arm
A thin and wiry arm that was obviously too long for the recipient and thus extended their reach.

Face
Charming eye
A goatlike, golden eye that allowed the recipient to see perfectly in even magical darkness and to charm other creatures.
Fearsome eye
A bright red eye with a black iris that allowed the recipient to see invisibility and to paralyze other creatures with fear.
Fiendish ear
A fan-shaped ear (looking like spikes connected by membranous skin) that granted the recipient keen hearing, except on rare occasions when all they heard was the chaos of Pandemonium.
Fiendish jaw
A heavy, tusked jawbone covered in scaled skin and full of sharp, crooked teeth. The recipient of this graft could use the heavy tusks to gore their enemies.

Legs
Fast leg
A long and agile leg that made the recipient faster.
Springing leg
An extremely long leg with a pronounced bend at the knee (which could be bent either forwards or backwards) that allowed the recipient to jump further and higher. The leg was awkward enough that the recipient had difficulty walking.
Strong leg
A large and muscular leg that enhanced the recipient's endurance.
Trampling leg
A short, thick, elephantlike leg that could be used to stomp on enemies.

Tails
Sting tail
A large, scorpionlike tail that ended in a poisonous stinger that could be used to attack foes.
Whip tail
A long, whiplike tail that seemed to have a mind of its own and could lash out at the recipient's enemies. Injuries from the tail burned with paralyzing pain.

Wings
Feathered wings
Large, birdlike wings tipped with claws—similar to those of an erinyes or vrock—enabled the recipient to fly.
Membranous wing
Batlike wings—similar to those of a pit fiend or succubus—enabled the recipient to fly.

Other
Fiendish skin
Grafts of thick skin that provided permanent natural armor.

Notable Owners
 Rauthan Zarass, a Zhentarim slaver and underling of Manshoon who had a clawed arm. He was slain by Storm Silverhand.
 Warduke, an infamous evil adventurer from Oerth, who possessed a glowing fearsome eye.

## Fighting doll

Fighting dolls were a type of wooden doll that were designed to be pitted in "fights" against one another. They originated in the Sembian capital of Ordulin, but increasingly became popular across the wider reaches of Faerûn in the 14 century DR, being played with as far away as the city of Waterdeep.

Description
These humanoid-shaped dolls were made up of hand-painted, finished pieces of fine Sembian wood joined together, with heads that could be knocked off, and stood  high. Each was typically outfitted with a small, sharply pointed sword and thin metal shield. They were distinguished from other dolls by a long wooden stick, hinged and mounted on its back, that was used to manipulate the doll. These gave the dolls movement and thus their namesake capability of "fighting" other fighting dolls.

A variation of these dolls made on the island nation of Lantan lacked these sticks, instead being semi-intelligent constructs of clockwork that could move of their own accord.While they are not stated in the original text to be constructs as such, the fact that the incident in Baldur's Gate is described as a "mutiny" implies a higher level of automation and intelligence above that of mere clockwork toys.

Availability
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold Sembian-made fighting dolls in pairs at a cost of 7 gold pieces, though discouraged purchasing them for any children under the age of 12 due to the sharpness of their included swords. The Catalogue also sold the clockwork variety for a time, though discontinued their sale after an incident that occurred in the city of Baldur's Gate.

History
In the years following their creation, passionate fans of fighting dolls in Ordulin developed higher, more masterful levels of play with the toys. The city became renowned for these "battle-masters", individuals who had raised fighting doll bouts to the level of an art form.

Sometime around the mid–14 century DR, a defective group of clockwork fighting dolls in Baldur's Gate that had been purchased from Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue staged a mutiny and escaped from their owners, running rampant through the city. Following this, the Catalogue's next publication included a note stating they had cancelled their next planned import shipment of said dolls and would be taking no further orders for them, though would be continuing to sell other forms of clockwork toys. The note also included a statement from Aurora, expressing deep regret over the trouble spawned by the dolls and that she was lending Baldur's Gate whatever resources she had available to ensure that they were all captured and destroyed.

## Fingers of Szass Tam

The Fingers of Szass Tam were five defensive towers placed equidistantly around the perimeter of Surcross in Thay.

Structure
The towers were tall and skinny cylinders, standing several hundreds of feet tall but only a few dozen feet wide. They were built from bone and marble, although several sections of the walls were magical, such that they only existed when viewed from the outside. These walls concealed rooms that thus had access to the outside, and allowed for arcane siege engines within the Fingers to fire on enemy targets. The towers had no other windows.

Interior
The towers served as defensive and tactical hubs for the Thayan soldiers of Surcross. They were connected via teleportation circles to the city's Garrison, allowing troops to quickly deploy into the Fingers.

Powers
Upon the command of a Thayan officer, each Finger could project a field of force. This could be used either to create bridges and ramps (allowing troops within the Fingers to directly deploy to anywhere in or immediately outside of Surcross) or to create shields to protect the city from aerial bombardment (including spells). Such shields were usually deployed to protect vulnerable locations, but the five Fingers were also capable of extending a shield over the entirety of Surcross (although this could only be maintained for a few minutes).

History
The towers were constructed sometime after the Spellplague caused the rising of the Thayan Plateau. Realizing that any enemies who sought to attack Thay proper were likely to use flying magic or vessels to scale the Plateau, Thayan military leaders concluded that Surcross required defenses to protect it from aerial attacks. Thus, these defensive towers were built and were named for Thay's regent, Szass Tam.

When the Netherese enclave Koltunral arrived to lay siege to Surcross in the, the Fingers of Szass Tam were the only thing that kept the city from being destroyed by Kolthunral's deadly meteor tubes.

## Field of Megiddo

The Field of Megiddo, also known as the Battlefield of Megiddo, was a plain on the Avernus layer of Baator. It was one of the many battlefields of the Blood War.

Geography
This battlefield was located somewhere close to Ankhwugaht Prime, a small divine realm in Avernus belonging to the Mulhorandi god Set.

History
Sometime in the late 14 century DR, a group of adventurers from Ravens Bluff found themselves in the plane of Baator, at a spot overlooking the Field of Megiddo, after being swept away by psychic winds in the Astral Plane and dragged through an anomaly known as Nelseddi's Maelstrom. At that time, a group of blue dragons were in flight from Megiddo, having taken a severe beating from tanar'ri, and they quickly sprung an attack upon the Ravenians. When the adventurers eventually managed to return to the Prime Material plane, they startled their benefactor Simon Regulus, who had just managed to find the Field of Megiddo in an ornate mirror he was scrying with.

Rumors & Legends
According to some legends, the Field of Megiddo was destined to one day be the site of the final battle of the Blood War.

Appendix
Background
The name of this location is likely a reference to Tel Megiddo, a location that according to Christian eschatology is set to be a battlefield for Armageddon (which is named for Megiddo).

## Firzu

Firzu was a shady merchant who sailed past the town of Reithwin, along the River Chionthar, sometime before the late 14 century DR.

History
Firzu was a notorious merchant who regularly tried to sail past Reithwin's toll house without paying the toll for the trade goods he transported. The toll house workers confiscated a silver and gold chalice from Firzu's shipment, but soon after, it disappeared from the vault to Gerringothe Thorm's disdain. Firzu's life ended sometime before the, when the town was plunged into the darkness and turned into the shadow-cursed lands. His charred body, still wearing an enchanted ring of trading, was stored in the House of Healing's morgue—likely murdered by the wicked Sharran Thorm family for avoiding the tolls.

## Fish Tails

Fish Tails was an old warehouse turned into a factory, just two doors down from the Ravensbridge on Karen Court.

The factory produced fried fishcakes made with various types of fish, with an occasional turtle, crab, and squid ending up in the mix. The factory workers minced, sauced, mixed, breaded, and fried the fish slop parts into hand-sized cakes that were considered to be good quality among other such fishcakes produced elsewhere. The factory workers insisted that no rats, cats or other non-marine life ever ended up in the foods produced in Fish Tails, despite what local tales claimed.

## First Falls

First Falls was a village in Thay that was the place where the Salamander War began.

Geography
The village was located within the tharch of Lapendrar. It sat just atop the First Escarpment along the River Lapendrar.

History
In the, the village was burned down by the salamanders and efreet summoned by the Zulkir of Conjuration when he opened a gate to the Elemental Plane of Fire, beginning the ill-fated Salamander War. Nevertheless, some of the survivors hoped to rebuild.

Notable Locations
The gate to the Plane of Fire was not permanent, but remained opened until after the destruction of the cities of Lasdur, Taskaunt, Murbant, and Thasselen. It lay on the opposite side of the ruined village from the river, and appeared simply as a  steady flame sitting in the middle of a burnt patch of grass. It was protected by an honor guard of salamanders.

Inhabitants
The people of First Falls subsisted on fishing and farming. They mostly worked in supporting local traveling tax collectors.

## Fish market (Skullport)

The Fish Market was a open-air market located in the Lower Port of Skullport.

Description
This small section of the Lower Port was dedicated to the sale of various seafood, including fish, mussels, and shellfish. Some of the delicacies were imported from the Lands Above, packed in ice or preserved in magical cooling chests, while others were sourced locally from the River Sargauth. To attract potential buyers, some fish were artfully displayed on beds of ice, while others were kept alive in buckets and containers filled with seawater or river water to ensure freshness at the point of sale. Despite these efforts, spoilage was common, and encounters with tainted seafood was a frequent and unfortunate souvenir for visitors to the market.

Defenses
The overpowering odor of fish, particularly from those being prepared and filleted, was often so strong that it attracted scavengers from the neighboring areas of Undermountain. To safeguard the market's stock, two to four mercenaries were stationed nearby, clad in studded leather armor and armed with spears. These guards were tasked with driving away or eliminating the occasional scrag that was drawn to the scent of the market's wares.

## Fishbone Tavern

Fishbone Tavern was a rough-and-tumble drinking establishment near Luskan's Open Shore docks.

Location
The tavern was located a few blocks away from the Open Shore (an area of the Whitesails Harbor). It was close enough to smell the sea air and hear the sea gulls.

The building was easily recognized by the hanging sign above the door, which bore the establishment's name as well as a real, rotting fish that was nailed to the board.

Interior
The inside was dark and cramped with only eight tables, each with stools for sitting. The long, dark-stained wooden bar dominated one side of the room, with oval plaques bearing yellowed fish skeletons adorning the wall behind it. The wall opposite the bar had a small, smoky fireplace.

Services
The tavern catered to local laborers and fishers. It was also a popular haunt and thus a front for Dran Enterprises.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, every dockworker in Luskan was familiar with the Fishbone Tavern.

By the, both the owner, Fryer, and bouncer, Zurb, worked with Dran Enterprises. Fryer spied on her clientele on their behalf while Zurb kept their enemies out of the tavern. Members of Dran Enterprises frequented the tavern, while regulars who were not affiliated with that faction included Milo, Rakeem, Tortuk, and Velos.

## Fish market (Port Nyanzaru)

The fish market of Port Nyanzaru, Chult, was a marketplace where the city's residents shopped for daily meals.

Location
The marketplace was located in the city's Market Ward on the border with the Harbor Ward. It sat between the feet of the Market Ward's two main hills: to the north of Yklwazi Hill and to the southwest of Mount Sibasa.

Services

The market was the premier place to buy daily fresh catches of fish and seafood in Port Nyanzaru. The city's fishing industry specialized in saltwater fish, such as tuna, but also sold some river fish, such as catfish, Esmel perch, and kapenta.

The selection was always better earlier in the day before it was picked over by customers, thus the highest quality (and most expensive) fish were sold in the mornings.

History
Fish and seafood were dietary staples in Port Nyanzaru, not only because fish was more appetizing than dinosaur, but because fishing was generally safer than hunting in the Chultan jungles. Furthermore, fishing in the Bay of Chult was safer than venturing up the Soshenstar or Tiryki rivers, so saltwater fish were more common at the market.

## Final struggle

Final struggle was a contingency evocation and necromancy spell of the 8 circle of magic used by evil wizards to avoid a certain demise and take over a new body.

Effects
After the spell was prepared and cast, it remained dormant until the caster was killed. Just like the contingency spell, it activated in a specific circumstance—the caster's death. Once they passed, the caster's spirit lingered in the area where their dead body was found. This spirit had one chance at returning to life: through a single use of a magic jar spell ingrained in their mind after the transformation into a spirit. The magic jar spell could be cast at any sentient creature within a distance of, or more if the caster was more powerful. If magic jar was successful, then the spirit immediately gained full possession of the victim, while the victim's life force was forcefully ejected and lost. In case the target resisted the magic jar spell, the necromancer simply died and went on into their afterlife.

Most successful castings of the final struggle were acts of true evil and turned any good- and neutral-aligned wizard towards a life of malice and destruction. However, in some rare cases that fate was avoided, for example, if the possessed sentient creature was hollow or fully willing to sacrifice themselves for the caster.

Components
The spell called for verbal, somatic, and material components worth at least 1,600 gp that were consumed by the casting. The spell was prepared and incanted over a period of eight hours.

History
The final struggle spells were foul works of evil and were used by villainous necromancers who were enemies of the Harpers. The knowledge of the spell was deemed dangerous and a copy of the spell was hidden away in one of the many secret Harper vaults. Being a bardic organization, they were unwilling to destroy knowledge, even such vile and evil knowledge. Harpers elected to hide the wondrous items and dark magics away and keep them under lock and key.

## Fireworks

Fireworks were a type of low explosive in the Realms designed for the purpose of entertainment and aesthetic displays. Those who made or handled fireworks were sometimes known as pyrobolists.

Description

Fireworks produced a sort of popping sound when they went off, usually in a rapid succession that could be heard from great distances, and dazzling displays of colors and lights in the sky. Through the use of dyes, different effects could be achieved. Some fireworks required a mortar in order to be shot into the air, while others flew by their own propulsion.

Smokepowder was used in the production of fireworks. More specifically, small, magically limited kernels known as fireseeds. These were what imparted fireworks their distinctive "popping" noise.

The smell of fireworks was compared by some to that of brimstone.

Availability
Across the Realms, fireworks were produced in Baldur's Gate, Lantan, Shou Lung, and in Waterdeep. The fireworks made in Shou Lung were exported all across Kara-tur and even farther afield to the Blade Kingdoms and western Faerûn's Sword Coast, though Shou fireworks were an uncommon sight in Waterdeep. Outside of Kara-Tur, fireworks were considered by some to be a rather Gnomish field of crafts, with some becoming quite devoted to their making. Most fireworks in Waterdeep were produced by rock gnomes.

In the city of Baldur's Gate, Felogyr's Fireworks had a monopoly on fireworks production, as its owners were the only Baldurians with a license to produce smokepowder, a state of affairs that went as far back as the shop's founding. In the late 1360s DR, the prices for fireworks ranged from 25 to 75 gold pieces.

Additionally, a number of Gondar temples produced and sold fireworks. Beyond manufacturers, fireworks were found to varying degrees in Calimshan, Delzimmer, Gana, Halruaa, the Blade Kingdoms, Neverwinter, Ravens Bluff, and Westgate, as well as in the City of Brass in the Plane of Fire,

Varieties
 Firecrackers, a type of small ground firework that was developed in Shou Lung.
 Oddfire, a brand of fireworks that used a unique mixture of powders to create particoloured explosions. Named for the company that produced them, Oddfire, they could be found for sale at Felogyr's Fireworks in the late 15 century DR.
 Twinkledust, a type of firework similar to firecrackers, but produced in the Blade Kingdoms.
 Whizbangs, a type of firework sold at Felogyr's Fireworks in the late 15 century DR.

Safety

Though fireworks were designed for recreational purposes, like any explosive they could become dangerous if misused or handled improperly, both to the person operating them and bystanders, ranging from burns to serious wounds. For safety it was often recommended that one wear gloves while handling them, to ignite them at arm's length, and to be mindful of wind direction.

Animals, such as dogs, could be frightened by the loud noise they produced.

Usage
Fireworks were often used at festivals and celebrations.
 In Thentia, fireworks featured in many periodic, popular festivals.Due to the phrasing of this source, it is unclear if the text is referring literally to fireworks or using the term descriptively for displays of magic.
 They were used as part of some Feast of the Moon celebrations in Baldur's Gate and Delzimmer.
 In Neverwinter, celebrations of the Shou festival Feast of Lanterns featured fireworks displays, which were organized by Far Eastern pyrobolists.
 In west Zakhara, fireworks were fired off in the city of Gana as part of the Festival of the Pearl.
 Small fireworks were used by House Baenre during celebrations.
 The Church of Berronar Truesilver used sparkler fireworks on their holy days, Midwinter day and Midsummer night, to mark the ending of each unison prayer.
 In Waterdeep, the Church of Mystra celebrated the holiday Gods' Day with a huge festival that culminated in a magical fireworks display that went on well into the night.
 A fireworks display was a common feature of the festival of Lliira's Night in the Sword Coast in the late 15 century DR, having been first popularized in Baldurian celebrations of the festival by Felogyr's Fireworks.

History
By 1357 DR, fireworks had been a common fixture in the nation of Shou Lung for several hundred years. Meanwhile, in the city of Westgate, they were considered to be a relatively rare sight around 1368 DR, being viewed as a luxury.

In the, the arcanaloth Yrkhetep impersonated Khan Kertep and led his barbarian army against the city of Hlath in Chondath. During the siege of Hlath, in the heat of battle against the city's gathered defenders, he set off fireworks to confuse and frighten the Faerûnians, counting on them having no experience with them.

In the, Iakhovas attacked Baldur's Gate with his army of sahuagin and other sea creatures as part of the Twelfth Serôs War. Adventurers Khlinat Ironeater and Jherek Wolf's-get managed to kill many sahuagin and repel the attack by detonating a wagon full of kegs of smokepowder and fireworks that they had gotten from Felogyr's Fireworks.

Celebratory fireworks became a common occurrence in the skies of Halruaa following the nation's victory against the Crinti in the .

Sometime in the, a disguised Imbralym Skoond visited Felogyr's Fireworks and bought everything it had in stock, as well as all of its raw materials, under the alias "Horus Kope", on behalf of Grand Duke Torlin Silvershield. Initially he sought to break Felogyr's monopoly on fireworks, having Skoond line up suppliers so he could arrange to produce more smokepowder, though his plans were derailed by Bhaal's growing influence on his mind. By 1492 DR, Felogyr's continued to have a monopoly over fireworks in the city.

Following the end of the High Sun Games, the downfall of Forge Fitzwilliam and Sofina, and the liberation of Neverwinter and the Neverwinter Wood in the late 1490s DR, the people of the area celebrated with a display of fireworks in the air between the city and the forest. These were watched by the adventurers responsible—Edgin Darvis, Holga Kilgore, Simon Aumar, Doric, and Kira Darvis—from a wood elf tree village in the forest.

## Fire marshes

The fire marshes were a spread of hazardous marshlands and peat bogs in the High Moor, lit with everburning flames that made them into dangerous, inhospitable areas.

Description
Once, the fire marshes were nothing but just regular wetlands, filled with peat and other decaying matter. But one man's attempt to exterminate a local population of trolls resulted in the peat being lit aflame with either permanent spells or magic items of some description, creating the "fire marshes." Adventurers stupid enough to travel across the fire marshes were in constant danger of tripping over a flame jet, sending forth a column of fire and hissing smoke. This magical flames were activated by any creatures found in the area. However, an application of the dispel magic spells could quell a small portion of the swamps, making it unable to produce flames for several minutes.

Geography
The fire marshes were spread across the breadth of the High Moor, east of the Trollbark Forest, southwest of the South Wood and north of the Dragonspear Castle.

Flora & Fauna
The fire marshes were home to many species of moss with sphagnum moss that died and turned into peat being amongst the most common. Some creatures were not bothered by the swamp's flammability and were found in the fire marshes, offering even more danger to those traversing them. These creatures and plants adapted to the flames, granting them an innate resistance to fire and ensured that they rarely were seen outside the fire marshes. Tanglers, a local variety of giant sundews of green body with pink splotches, was not bothered by the acidic swamp nor by the flames. The marshes were also home to fyreflies and various species of mundane and giant dragonflies – dangerous predators that came in many colors, metallic being the most prevalent. Other creatures of fire nature that called the bogs home were fire toads, fire lizards, and pyrohydrae. Planar creatures of fire were also not an uncommon sight, such as hell hounds, fire snakes, fire mephits, fire elementals and salamanders.

Other creatures that were occasionally seen in the fire marshes were black dragons, fire bats, rabbits, moon rats, swamp ferrets, leeches, will-o'-wisps, frogs, kestrels, owls, as well as undead as they did not  trigger flame eruptions (young and ancient Netherese wights, zombies, skeletons, ghouls, haunts, phantoms).

History
Sometime during the life of Daeros Dragonspear, a 13 century half-dwarven adventurer, he thought it to be a good idea to fight the ever-growing population of trolls in the High Moor with fire, creating an environmental disaster and wrecking an ecology at the heart of the Moor. The half-dwarven adventurer and his friends created spells or used some unknown magic items to create sporadic flame bursts in the peat bogs to kill or at least drive the trolls away. The plan worked too well, killing many trolls but the fire magics used had permanency and turned the bogs into the deadly fire marshes.

In the late 14 century DR, a goblin tribe near the fire marshes began worshiping a salamander that resided there. The planar creature demanded increased amounts of meat from its worshipers. These stupid goblins lived to the south of the bogs and became a great danger, attacking anything and everything.

Notable Inhabitants
One notable inhabitant of the fire marshes was a pit fiend, some believed to be called Deref. This hideous  tall monster occasionally tried to wrangle up an army of fire creatures and humanoids in the marshes from time to time.

## Fists of Zuoken

The Fists of Zuoken  were a famous monastic order of psionic martial artists dedicated to achieving enlightenment through mastery of both physical and mental development, constantly striving towards personal perfection. They were also devoted to the causes of finding and guiding potential psionics users, as well as safeguarding all those who wielded the Invisible Art from persecution.

Organization
This order was affiliated with Zuoken,It is left somewhat ambiguous as to whether or not the Fists of Zuoken were specifically a religious organization. While certainly named for and inspired by him, the information provided on the order in Expanded Psionics Handbook and Psionic Power does not paint them as strictly Zuokenai. an Oerthian demigod of psionics, martial arts, and monks. Their name came from his holy symbol, which was a striking fist. According to legend he was once a human martial artist, who several centuries ago ascended to godhood after achieving mental and physical perfection, that being the highest level of skill possible in edel (psionics) and da'shon ("falling hail", a complicated Oerthian martial art).

The leader of the fists, referred to as the scion of Zouken, was peer-selected among those who were considered the nearest to enlightenment and therefore most embodied the qualities of Zuoken. Eight elder monks assisted the scion in managing the order and developing its curriculum of exercises, practices, and rituals that were meant to strengthen each aspect of the self. These monks were divided into two groups, the Masters of the Wind and the Masters of the Seasons. The former were focused on physical studies, while the latter were responsible for the order's mental exercises and scholastic studies. The scion of Zouken was often the most eldest disciple, derived from one of the two groups of elder monks.

Those who took up the path of a Fist of Zuoken were typically monks, often ones with a wild talent, though other types of psionics users were not restricted from joining their ranks. The majority of the order's members were of a good alignment, with a significant minority of lawful good and neutral disciples. Because of their various studies into the legendary exploits of Zuoken, it was not uncommon for members to know the Primordial language.

Activities
Fists often took up jobs as guards at monasteries that practiced psionic learning, such as Zuokenai temples, and even operated some of their own small monasteries. Many others took up the life of an adventurer, believing that the exploration, investigation, survival, and true combat that such a life entailed would more keenly hone their abilities than any amount of exercise or study. Above all else, their creed to protect psionics users and psionic creatures from persecution took top priority for Fists of Zuoken, and the life of an adventurer offered disciples more opportunity to aid those in need.

As they believed only strict monastic training could lead to enlightenment, disciples worked to coax those psionics users who were more fueled by passion, such as ardents and battleminds, towards taking up a more disciplined path of study.

Dogma
The Fists of Zuoken believed that all living creatures were innately linked to psychic energy, but that only those with the potential for
enlightenment could harness that energy. And even then, only by obtaining a measure of enlightenment – unity of the mind, body, and soul – could one hope to master the Invisible Art. Ergo, psionics users were the only mortals capable of achieving true enlightenment and reaching a greater existence following death. This system of belief encouraged the nurturing of mental powers in all who possessed them. While a disciple was responsible for their own development, helping others could aid a psionics user on this journey toward self-awareness and enlightenment.

When it came to the various unifying theories regarding psionics and psychic energy, members of the order generally leaned towards them being gifts of the gods or the belief that psionics users were the next evolutionary step of the mortal races.

Abilities
Fists of Zuoken drew from the same well of psionic powers as psychic warriors. Like other psionics users, any powers they learned became ingrained in their minds and didn't need to prepared, like how some spellcasters needed to do for their spells. In addition to mental powers, members displayed the formidable martial art skills that one could expect from monks.

Notable Members
 Blaen Haltspear, a ghostwise halfling disciple who hailed from the Channathwood of south Faerûn.

## Firebush spice paste

Firebush spice paste was a condiment made from the fireberries, a species of spicy greenish peppers that grew on a bush with distinctly red leaves in the Battle of Bones of the Western Heartlands.

Description
There were two notable recipes for firebush spice paste. The first version called for frying up chopped onions and garlic in oil with a small handful of fireberries added in later. Once the peppers darkened in the pan, a love-apple was diced in and the concoction was sprinkled with salt, sugar, and molasses. The paste then was slowly cooked until oil began to pool on top. This paste then was jarred and kept for a couple of month or much longer if kept refrigerated. The spice was potent and a pinch was enough to flavor a big bowl of soup, stew, or rice.

The second version called for soy sauce exported out of Shou Lung. This recipe required no cooking and as such, it was quite popular. The mix was comprised of four ounces of soy or sho-yu, crushed garlic, a couple of crushed firebush berries, a teaspoon of molasses. When combined, it was left to marinate for at least half an hour. Unlike the cooked version, the condiment lasted indefinitely once jarred and the spice levels increased with the sauce's age.

History
In the late 14 century DR, two recipes of firebush spice paste were recorded and popularized by Bronwyn Starglade.

## Fields of Oriam

The Fields of Oriam were plains south of the Bay of Chessenta in Chessenta.

Geography
They formed a savannah of rolling grassland with long grasses and occasional pine barrens, but the soil was poor, being too dry and acidic for farmland.

Inhabitants
Hardly any human settlements were to be found here, partly because of the limited agriculture but mostly because the area is home to cockatrices.

Locations
Ancient Untheric ruins dotted the savannah. The most notable among these is the so-called Lost Temple of Ptah, which was said to still hold holy tablets of magnificent power and wisdom.

## Finality, the Fall of the Sun

Finality, the Fall of the Sun was a powerful short sword found in the ruins of Myth Drannor in the late 14 century DR.

Powers
Made of adamantine, Finality could cut through almost any material. Though empowered by the vitality of the sun, the blade was turned towards death as well. It burned foes with a fierce internal flame, as well as draining vitality and simply snuffing out the life of those it struck.

History
The blade originated in old Myth Drannor from before the Weeping War, and was originally created to honor the sun. Following the fall of the city, it was corrupted to represent the final sunset instead. It eventually made its way to an old Cult of the Dragon base hidden in the ruins, which was then taken over by a Red Wizards of Thay strike force that sought to capture the dracolich Karliath. The extraordinary blade may have then been taken by the forces of the Cormanthor Elves when they attacked the Red Wizards while claiming the ruins.

## Fire minion

Fire minions were fearsome creatures from the Elemental Plane of Fire, some of whom had migrated to the sun in Realmspace.

Description
Standing some  tall, their bodies seemed to be made entirely of fire, constantly swirling and flickering with living light. They could assume any form they wanted but the most common preferred form was that of a humanoid with fangs and horns.

Combat
Fire minions would fight by rising out of the fiery ground, taking their forms in the process. This meant that they seldom actually took their foes by surprise, but did make for a more elegant entrance, which apparently was more important for the fire minions themselves. In battle, the fire minions could use the selfsame ability to retreat underground and either reappear somewhere more advantageous or flee from the combat entirely.

The weapon of choice for most fire minions was a large two-handed sword, which they used alongside their constant flame aura that burnt everyone not immune to fire within . As they themselves were made from seemingly living flame, they were immune to all fire-based attacks, and magical flames such as fireballs or even dragon breath would heal them rather than hurt them. With such fury did their fire burn that even water-based attacks would nearly be turned away. Only cold magics could truly hurt them, and seriously, as their fiery bodies and their very lives would quickly be extinguished.

Society
Fire, flame, and lava were the natural homes of fire minions, who traveled through these materials as easily as walking. However they had only a limited ability to leave those environs and to leave the flames came with a risk of injury or even death. If they did not return to a place of fire and flame after 6 hours, their flame aura would disappear and their bodies started breaking down, until they either returned to flames or they perished as dry burnt-out husks evaporating in clouds of ash.

Little was known about the normal lives of fire minions, as they were not a society open to outsiders, except that they came from the Plane of Fire and were considered free-willed fire elementals by almost all sages. They had a limited ability to gate themselves to the Prime Material plane, but only in areas of the most intense flames, like those found in the calderas of volcanoes, or on the surface of the sun. As such, they were only encountered in the most exotic of locales.

History
Groups of fire minions were observed to live on the surface of the sun, but due to the properties of that surface had never been contacted by any humans or demihumans.

## Fizben the Magnificent

Fizben the Magnificent was the alias of an adventuring human bard and minstrel who claimed to be very famous in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Fizben was a friendly man with "passable good looks" and a good singing voice. The adventurer possessed impressive charisma of an experienced actor. In the late 1480s DR, Fizben appeared to be in his forties.

History
Little was known about Fizben the Magnificent's past, not even where he was born. However, it was quite obvious that the name was just an alias, and his body language and skills of a performer were evidence of his past prominence. The man kept his past a secret and this secret was the likely reason for his wayfaring lifestyle and the use of a fake name. By the late 15 century DR, Fizben was known for his clever witticisms and a number of original ballads.

According to Blackfeather, a Harper agent, Fizben the Magnificent frequented "singing clubs" in Eshpurta and Darromar—establishments that offered traveling minstrels overnight lodgings, watered down house wine, and scant meals, in exchange for performing five or more songs for the guests.

Appendix
Behind the Scenes
Fizben the Magnificent was one of the player characters used by Jason Snelson circa 2018-2020. In the original campaign that began with Lost Mine of Phandelver, Fizben was a halfling. With Jason's passing in 2024, in his memory, his character was included in The Forgotten Realms Wiki Presents Juniper's Companion to Venturesome Cookery with the lore graciously provided by Ed Greenwood.

## Fitzallus

Fitzallus the Footpad was a Sembian bard, the first owner of Harold, a powerful enchanted heavy crossbow, who died some time before the late 14 century DR.

History
When Fitzallus commissioned his enchanted crossbow, he endlessly praised the new weapon. His excitement over the crossbow was such that his friends began teasing the bard, saying that the weapon was Fitzallus's new "best friend" and he needed to give it an appropriate name. When the enchanted weapon was ready, Fitzallus proudly strolled into a tavern with a bright smile and announced to his friends the weapon's name – Harold.

Several months later, Fitzallus the Footpad perished in a burglary. According to his will, Harold was to be buried with the bard, yet the crossbow disappeared before the funeral.

Sometime later, by the, Fitzallus's crossbow ended up in the hands of Rhynwis, the head of a fledgling thieves guild on Sword Coast North.

## Fist of Rundeen

The Fist of Rundeen was an elite band of assassins that served the Rundeen mercantile organization in the mid-to-late 14 century DR and active in the Shining Sea region.

Organization
Sponsored and directed by the Rundeen, the Fist and Rundeen were controlled directly by the First Yrshelem, the second tier of Rundeen leadership, but technically ranked below the Mitalibbar, their collectors and watchers, like their other subsidiaries.

Membership
The Fist of Rundeen comprised only eight active members, no more and no less.Cloak & Dagger p. 130 instead says the Fist of Rundeen has twelve members, but this is presumed to be in error, as it gives only a brief mention and seems to miscount the active and former members listed in Empires of the Shining Sea pp. 172–173. Alternatively, this counts inactive members or it has been increased for some reason. All of them were dedicated assassins, killers, and warriors trained as fighters and rogues. They were entirely loyal to their paymasters and utterly merciless on completing their missions. But they were much less loyal to one another: el Scimitar slew three over 1365–1370 and el Yadhi plotted to be rid of el Dyram because she desired his position. Moreover, retirement was impossible; they were members for life, or at least as long as they survived.

Names held vital significance to them. Their real names were kept closely guarded secrets, never mentioned even amongst the Rundeen and very rarely amongst one another. Members instead adopted noms de guerre in Alzhedo based on their preferred weapons, and used these names with the Rundeen and one another. In contrast, they had a tendency to name their favored weapons.

Tactics
Each had an unique weapon of choice, one not wielded by another active member.

Activities
Forming a small but elite strike force, they were used to eliminate threats and traitors to the Rundeen when deemed absolutely necessary. They were dispatched on missions involving committing murder or causing havoc.

Relationships
The Fist of Rundeen were one of a number of mercenary and adventuring bands, along with the Clenched Fist and Many Long Arms, formed by the Rundeen with the express purpose of opposing the Harpers.

History
The Fist of Rundeen were formed some time in the 11 century DR. The following centuries would see over a thousand members join the band, but never more or less than eight at a time.

Circa 1338 DR, the man known as el Yadhi joined the Fist of Rundeen. For 32 years, he would outlive all others and rise to leadership of the band.

They had several clashes with the Harpers, but were defeated several times by them, who saw them as no longer effective by 1367 DR.

Members
Known members of the Fist of Rundeen circa 1370 DR were, in order of seniority:
 el Yadhi: Called the Hand, a human fighter with a penchant for snapping necks and crushing skulls, he was the leader and longest-lived of the band, with skills in tactics and planning.
 el Scimitar: Called the Scimitar, a human fighter and brother of el Jambiya; he'd killed three fellow members to avenge perceived insults to her.
 el Jambiya: Called the Dagger, a human thief who considered herself an artist with the blade.
 el Katar: Called the Knife, a human thief and giggling madman who delighted in terrorizing victims.
 el Kholnar: Called the Axe, a half-orc fighter and former executioner for a pasha.
 el Dyram: Called the Arm, a human fighter and former gladiator of the Arena Efreetum who earned her name there before joining the Fist.

Former known members included el Jikholnar (the Hand Axe), el Enaza (the Spear), el Nadim (the Club), el Cestys (the Cestus/Spiked Knuckles) and el Nataf (the Cord/Garrote).
